      <p class="Titlepageinfo"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Abstract:</span></p>
      <p class="Abstract"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This document
      indicates the policy assertions for use with [<a href="#wspolicy">WS-Policy</a>]
      which apply to WSS: SOAP Message Security [<a href="#wss10">WSS10</a>, <a
      href="#wss11">WSS11</a>], [<a href="#wstrust">WS-Trust</a>] and [<a
      href="#wssecureconversation">WS-SecureConversation</a>]</span></p>
      <p class="Titlepageinfo"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Status:</span></p>
      <p class="Abstract"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This document
      was last revised or approved by the WS-SX TC on the above date. The level of
      approval is also listed above. Check the current location noted above for possible
      later revisions of this document. This document is updated periodically on no
      particular schedule.</span></p>
      <p class="Abstract"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Technical
      Committee members should send comments on this specification to the Technical
      Committee&rsquo;s email list. Others should send comments to the Technical
      Committee by using the &ldquo;Send A Comment&rdquo; button on the Technical
      Committee&rsquo;s web page at <a
      href="http://www.oasis-open.org/committees/ws-sx">http://www.oasis-open.org/committees/ws-sx</a><span
       class="MsoHyperlink">.</span></span></p>
      <p class="Abstract"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>For information
      on whether any patents have been disclosed that may be essential to implementing
      this specification, and any offers of patent licensing terms, please refer to the
      Intellectual Property Rights section of the Technical Committee web page (<a
      href="http://www.oasis-open.org/committees/ws-sx/ipr.php">http://www.oasis-open.org/committees/ws-sx/ipr.php</a><span
       class="MsoHyperlink">)</span>.</span></p>
      <p class="Abstract"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The
      non-normative errata page for this specification is located at <a
      href="http://www.oasis-open.org/committees/ws-sx">http://www.oasis-open.org/committees/ws-sx</a><span
       class="MsoHyperlink">.</span></span></p>

      rights will at any time be complete, or that any claims in such list are, in fact,
      Essential Claims.</span></p>

    <div class="Section2">
      <div style='border:none;border-top:solid gray 1.0pt;padding:6.0pt 0in 0in 0in'>
        <h1><a id="_Toc161826495" name="_Toc161826495"><span
        style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>1<span
        style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</span></span>
        <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Introduction</span></a></h1>
      </div>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>WS-Policy defines a
      framework for allowing web services to express their constraints and requirements.
      Such constraints and requirements are expressed as policy assertions. This document
      defines a set of security policy assertions for use with the [<a
      href="#wspolicy">WS-Policy</a>] framework with respect to security features
      provided in WSS: SOAP Message Security [<a href="#wss10">WSS10</a>, <a
      href="#wss11">WSS11</a>], [<a href="#wstrust">WS-Trust</a>] and [<a
      href="#wssecureconversation">WS-SecureConversation</a>].</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The assertions defined within this
      specification have been designed to work independently of a specific version of
      WS-Policy. At the time of the publication of this specification the versions of
      WS-Policy known to correctly compose with this specification are WS-Policy 1.2 and
      1.5. Within this specification the use of the namespace prefix wsp refers
      generically to the WS-Policy namespace, not a specific version.</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This document takes the approach of
      defining a base set of assertions that describe how messages are to be secured.
      Flexibility with respect to token types, cryptographic algorithms and mechanisms
      used, including using transport level security is part of the design and allows for
      evolution over time. The intent is to provide enough information for compatibility
      and interoperability to be determined by web service participants along with all
      information necessary to actually enable a participant to engage in a secure
      exchange of messages.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'><br />
      Sections 11, 12 and all examples and all Appendices are non-normative.</span></p>
      <h2><a id="_Toc161826496" name="_Toc161826496"></a><a id="_Toc104103910"
      name="_Toc104103910"><span style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>1.1</span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Example</span></a></h2>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Table 1 shows
      an</span> <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>"</span><span
      style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>Effective Policy</span><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>"</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>example, including binding assertions and
      associated property assertions, token assertions and integrity and confidentiality
      assertions. This example has a scope of [Endpoint Policy Subject], but for brevity
      the attachment mechanism is not shown.</span></p>
      <p class="MsoCaption"><a id="_Ref102231935" name="_Ref102231935"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Table</span></a> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>1</span><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>: Example security policy.</span></p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(01) &lt;wsp:Policy xmlns:wsp="..." xmlns:sp="..."&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(02) &nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;sp:SymmetricBinding&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(03) &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;wsp:Policy&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(04)
      &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;sp:ProtectionToken&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(05)
      &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;wsp:Policy&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(06)
      &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;sp:Kerberos
      sp:IncludeToken=".../IncludeToken/Once" /&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(07)
      &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;wsp:Policy&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(08)
      &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;sp:WSSKerberosV5ApReqToken11/&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(09)
      &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;wsp:Policy&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(10)
      &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;/sp:Kerberos&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(11)
      &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;/wsp:Policy&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(12)
      &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;/sp:ProtectionToken&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(13)
      &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;sp:SignBeforeEncrypting /&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(14)
      &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;sp:EncryptSignature /&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(15) &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;/wsp:Policy&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(16) &nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;/sp:SymmetricBinding&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(17) &nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;sp:SignedParts&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(18) &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;sp:Body/&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(19) &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;sp:Header<br />
      &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
      Namespace="http://schemas.xmlsoap.org/ws/2004/08/addressing"<br />
      &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; /&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(20) &nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;/sp:SignedParts&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(21) &nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;sp:EncryptedParts&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(22) &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;sp:Body/&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(23) &nbsp;&nbsp;&lt;/sp:EncryptedParts&gt;</p>
      <p class="CodeNumbered">(24) &lt;/wsp:Policy&gt;</p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Line 1 in Table 1
      indicates that this is a policy statement and that all assertions contained by the
      wsp:Policy element are required to be satisfied. Line 2 indicates the kind of
      security binding in force. Line 3 indicates a nested</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Courier New"'>wsp:Policy</span> <span
      style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>element which contains assertions that
      qualify the behavior of the SymmetricBinding assertion. Line 4 indicates a
      ProtectionToken assertion. Line 5 indicates a nested</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Courier New"'>wsp:Policy</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>element which contains assertions
      indicating the type of token to be used for the ProtectionToken. Lines 6 to 10
      indicate that a Kerberos V5 APREQ token is to be used by both parties in a message
      exchange for protection. Line 13 indicates that signatures are generated over
      plaintext rather than ciphertext. Line 14 indicates that the signature over the
      signed messages parts is required to be encrypted. Lines 17-20 indicate which
      message parts are to be covered by the primary signature; in this case the
      soap:Body element, indicated by Line 18 and any SOAP headers in the WS-Addressing
      namespace, indicated by line 19. Lines 21-23 indicate which message parts are to be
      encrypted; in this case just the soap:Body element, indicated by Line
      22</span><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>.</span></p>
      <h2><a id="_Toc104103913" name="_Toc104103913"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>1.2</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></a><a id="_Toc161826497"
      name="_Toc161826497"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Namespaces</span></a></h2>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The XML namespace
      URI that MUST be used by implementations of this specification is:</span></p>
      <div
      style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom: solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in; background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>
        <p class="Code" style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>
        http://docs.oasis-open.org/ws-sx/ws-securitypolicy/200702</p>
      </div>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Table 2 lists
      XML namespaces that are used in this specification. The choice of any namespace
      prefix is arbitrary and not semantically significant.</span></p>
      <p class="MsoCaption"><a id="_Ref99536081" name="_Ref99536081"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Table</span></a> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>2</span><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>: Prefixes and XML Namespaces used in this
      specification.</span></p>
      <table class="MsoNormalTable" border="1" cellspacing="0" cellpadding="0"
      style='border-collapse:collapse;border:none'>
        <tr>
          <td valign="top"
          style='border:solid windowtext 1.0pt;padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
            <p class="text0"><b><span
            style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Prefix</span></b></p>
          </td>
          <td width="393" valign="top"
          style='width:294.8pt;border:solid windowtext 1.0pt; border-left:none;padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
            <p class="text0"><b><span
            style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Namespace</span></b></p>
          </td>
          <td width="181" valign="top"
          style='width:135.9pt;border:solid windowtext 1.0pt; border-left:none;padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
            <p class="text0"><b><span
            style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Specification(s)</span></b></p>
          </td>
        </tr>
        <tr>
          <td valign="top"
          style='border:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-top:none; padding:0in 5.4pt 0in 5.4pt'>
            <p class="text0"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>S</span></p>
          </td>
          <td width="393" valign="top"

      <h2><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>1.3</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;<a id="_Toc161826498"
      name="_Toc161826498">Schema Files</a></span></h2>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A normative
      copy of the XML Schema [<a href="#xmlschema1">XML-Schema1</a>, <a
      href="#xmlschema2">XML-Schema2</a>] description for this specification can be
      retrieved from the following address:</span></p>
      <div
      style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom: solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in; background:#D9D9D9'>
        <p class="Code" style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>
        http://docs.oasis-open.org/ws-sx/ws-securitypolicy/200702/<span
        style='color:black'>ws-securitypolicy-1.2.xsd</span></p>
      </div>
      <h2><a id="_Toc161826499" name="_Toc161826499"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>1.4</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Terminology</span></a></h2>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><span class="LabelEmbedded"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Policy</span></span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>- <span class="TextChar1"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A collection of policy
      alternatives</span></span>.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Policy
      Alternative</span></b> <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>- A
      collection of policy assertions.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Policy
      Assertion</span></b> <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>- An individual
      requirement, capability, other property, or a behavior.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><b><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Initiator</span></b> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>- The role sending the initial message in
      a message exchange.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><b><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Recipient</span></b> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>- The targeted role to process the initial
      message in a message exchange.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Security
      Binding</span></b> <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>- A set of
      properties that together provide enough information to secure a given message
      exchange.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Security
      Binding Property</span></b> <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>- A
      particular aspect of securing an exchange of messages.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Security
      Binding Assertion</span></b> <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>- A
      policy assertion that identifies the type of security binding being used to secure
      an exchange of messages.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Security
      Binding Property Assertion</span></b><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>- <span class="TextChar1"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A policy assertion that specifies a
      particular value for a particular aspect of securing an exchange of
      message</span></span>.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Assertion
      Parameter</span></b> <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>- An element of
      variability within a policy assertion.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Token
      Assertion</span></b> <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>-Describes a
      token requirement. Token assertions defined within a security binding are used to
      satisfy protection requirements.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Supporting
      Token</span></b> <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>- A token used to
      provide additional claims.</span></p>
      <h3><a id="_Toc161826500" name="_Toc161826500"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>1.4.1</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Notational Conventions</span></a></h3>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The key words
      &ldquo;MUST&rdquo;, &ldquo;MUST NOT&rdquo;, &ldquo;REQUIRED&rdquo;,
      &ldquo;SHALL&rdquo;, &ldquo;SHALL NOT&rdquo;, &ldquo;SHOULD&rdquo;, &ldquo;SHOULD
      NOT&rdquo;, &ldquo;RECOMMENDED&rdquo;, &ldquo;MAY&rdquo;, and
      &ldquo;OPTIONAL&rdquo; in this document are to be interpreted as described in [<a
      href="#rfc2119">RFC2119</a>].</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This
      specification uses the following syntax to define outlines for
      assertions:</span></p>
      <ul style='margin-top:0in' type="disc">
        <li class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The syntax
        appears as an XML instance, but values in italics indicate data types instead of
        literal values.</span></li>
        <li class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Characters
        are appended to elements and attributes to indicate cardinality:</span></li>
        <li style="list-style: none">
          <ul style='margin-top:0in' type="circle">
            <li class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>"?" (0
            or 1)</span></li>
            <li class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>"*" (0
            or more)</span></li>
            <li class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>"+" (1
            or more)</span></li>
          </ul>
        </li>
        <li class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The
        character "|" is used to indicate a choice between alternatives.</span></li>
        <li class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The
        characters "(" and ")" are used to indicate that contained items are to be
        treated as a group with respect to cardinality or choice.</span></li>
        <li class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The
        characters "[" and "]" are used to call out references and property
        names.</span></li>
        <li class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Ellipses
        (i.e., "...") indicate points of extensibility. Additional children and/or
        attributes MAY be added at the indicated extension points but MUST NOT contradict
        the semantics of the parent and/or owner, respectively. By default, if a receiver
        does not recognize an extension, the receiver SHOULD ignore the extension;
        exceptions to this processing rule, if any, are clearly indicated
        below.</span></li>
        <li class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>XML
        namespace prefixes (see Table 2) are used to indicate the namespace of the
        element being defined.</span></li>
      </ul>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif";color:black'>Elements and Attributes
      defined by this specification are referred to in the text of this document using
      XPath 1.0 expressions. Extensibility points are referred to using an extended
      version of this syntax:</span></p>
      <ul style='margin-top:0in' type="disc">
        <li class="MsoNormal" style='color:black'><span
        style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>An element extensibility point is
        referred to using {any} in place of the element name. This indicates that any
        element name can be used, from any namespace other than the namespace of this
        specification.</span></li>
        <li class="MsoNormal" style='color:black'><span
        style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>An attribute extensibility point is
        referred to using @{any} in place of the attribute name. This indicates that any
        attribute name can be used, from any namespace other than the namespace of this
        specification.</span></li>
      </ul>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Extensibility
      points in the exemplar may not be described in the corresponding text.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>In this document
      reference is made to the</span> <span class="CodeChar"><span
      style='font-size: 9.0pt'>wsu:Id</span></span><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>attribute and the</span> <span
      class="CodeEmbedded">wsu:Created</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>and</span> <span
      class="CodeEmbedded">wsu:Expires</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>elements in a utility schema (</span><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'><a href="https://docs.oasis-open.org/wss/2004/01/oasis-200401-wss-wssecurity-utility-1.0.xsd">
      http://docs.oasis-open.org/wss/2004/01/oasis-200401-wss-wssecurity-utility-1.0.xsd</a></span><span
       style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>). The</span> <span
      class="CodeChar"><span style='font-size:9.0pt'>wsu:Id</span></span><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>attribute and the</span> <span
      class="CodeEmbedded">wsu:Created</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>and</span> <span
      class="CodeEmbedded">wsu:Expires</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>elements were added to the utility schema
      with the intent that other specifications requiring such an ID type attribute or
      timestamp element could reference it (as is done here).</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>WS-SecurityPolicy is
      designed to work with the general Web Services framework including WSDL service
      descriptions, UDDI businessServices and bindingTemplates and <a
      href="#soap">SOAP</a> message structure and message processing model, and
      WS-SecurityPolicy should be applicable to any version of <a href="#soap">SOAP</a>.
      The current SOAP</span> <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>1.2</span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>namespace URI is used herein to
      provide detailed examples, but there is no intention to limit the applicability of
      this specification to a single version of <a href="#soap">SOAP</a>.</span></p>
      <h2><a id="_Toc161826501" name="_Toc161826501"></a><a id="_Toc12011611"

      style='margin-top:4.0pt;margin-right:0in;margin-bottom:0in; margin-left:153.0pt;margin-bottom:.0001pt'>

      <p class="Ref"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <h2><a id="_Toc161826502" name="_Toc161826502"></a><a id="xmlsignature"
      name="xmlsignature"></a><a id="wslicense" name="wslicense"></a><a id="wsreferral"
      name="wsreferral"></a><a id="wsrouting" name="wsrouting"></a><a id="wssecurity"
      name="wssecurity"></a><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>1.6</span>
      <span style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>Non-Normative
      References</span></h2>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>None.</span></p>
      <p class="Ref" style='margin-left:0in;text-indent:0in'><a id="xmlc14n"
      name="xmlc14n"></a><a id="xmlencrypt" name="xmlencrypt"></a><a id="xmlns"
      name="xmlns"></a><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <div style='border:none;border-top:solid gray 1.0pt;padding:6.0pt 0in 0in 0in'>
        <h1><a id="_Toc161826503" name="_Toc161826503"></a><a id="_Toc104103917"
        name="_Toc104103917"></a><a id="_Toc99269229" name="_Toc99269229"></a><a
        id="_Toc96244132" name="_Toc96244132"></a><a id="_Toc95877078"
        name="_Toc95877078"></a><a id="_Toc95113086" name="_Toc95113086"></a><a
        id="_Toc85362758" name="_Toc85362758"></a><a id="_Toc85350204"
        name="_Toc85350204"></a><a id="_Toc73939789" name="_Toc73939789"><span
        style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>2<span
        style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</span></span>
        <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Security Policy
        Model</span></a></h1>
      </div>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This specification
      defines policy assertions for the</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>security properties for Web services.
      These assertions are primarily designed to represent</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>the security characteristics defined in
      the <a href="#wssecurity">WSS: SOAP Message Security</a> [<a
      href="#wss10">WSS10</a>] [<a href="#wss10">WSS11</a>], [<a
      href="#wstrust">WS-Trust</a>] and [<a
      href="#wssecureconversation">WS-SecureConversation</a>] specifications</span><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>, but they can also be used for describing
      security requirements at a more general or transport-independent level.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The primary goal of
      this specification is to define an initial set of patterns or sets of assertions
      that represent common ways to describe how messages are secured on a communication
      path.&nbsp; The intent is to allow flexibility in terms of the tokens,
      cryptography, and mechanisms used, including leveraging transport security, but to
      be specific enough to ensure interoperability based on assertion
      matching.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>It is a goal of the
      security policy model to leverage the WS-Policy framework&rsquo;s intersection
      algorithm for selecting policy alternatives and the attachment mechanism for
      associating policy assertions with web service artifacts.&nbsp; Consequently,
      wherever possible, the security policy assertions do not use parameters or
      attributes. This enables first-level, QName based assertion matching without
      security domain-specific knowledge to be done at the framework level. The first
      level matching is intended to provide a narrowed set of policy alternatives that
      are shared by the two parties attempting to establish a secure communication
      path.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>In general,
      assertions defined in this specification allow additional attributes, based on
      schemas, to be added on to the assertion element as an extensibility mechanism but
      the WS-Policy framework will not match based on these attributes. Attributes
      specified on the assertion element that are not defined in this specification or in
      WS-Policy are to be treated as informational properties.</span></p>
      <h2><a id="_Toc161826504" name="_Toc161826504"></a><a id="_Toc104103918"
      name="_Toc104103918"></a><a id="_Toc99269230" name="_Toc99269230"></a><a
      id="_Toc96244133" name="_Toc96244133"></a><a id="_Toc95877079"
      name="_Toc95877079"></a><a id="_Toc95113087" name="_Toc95113087"></a><a
      id="_Toc85362759" name="_Toc85362759"></a><a id="_Toc85350205"
      name="_Toc85350205"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>2.1</span> <span
      style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>Security Assertion Model</span></a></h2>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The goal to provide
      richer semantics for combinations of security constraints and requirements and
      enable first-level QName matching, is enabled by the assertions defined in this
      specification being separated into simple patterns: what parts of a message are
      being secured (Protection Assertions), general aspects or pre-conditions of the
      security (Conditional Assertions), the security mechanism (Security Binding
      Assertions) that is used to provide the security, the token types and usage
      patterns (Supporting Token Assertions) used to provide additional claims, and token
      referencing and trust options (WSS and Trust Assertions).</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>To indicate the
      scope of protection, assertions identify message parts that are to be protected in
      a specific way, such as integrity or confidentiality protection, and are referred
      to as protection assertions.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The general aspects
      of security includes the relationships between or characteristics of the
      environment in which security is being applied, such as the tokens being used,
      which are for integrity or confidentiality protection and which are supporting, the
      applicable algorithms to use, etc.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The security binding
      assertion is a logical grouping which defines how the general aspects are used to
      protect the indicated parts.&nbsp; For example, that an asymmetric token is used
      with a digital signature to provide integrity protection, and that parts are
      encrypted with a symmetric key which is then encrypted using the public key of the
      recipient.&nbsp; At its simplest form, the security binding restricts what can be
      placed in the</span> <span style='font-family:"Courier New"'>wsse:Security</span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>header and the associated processing
      rules.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The intent of
      representing characteristics as assertions is so that QName matching will be
      sufficient to find common alternatives and so that many aspects of security can be
      factored out and re-used.&nbsp; For example, it may be common that the mechanism is
      constant for an endpoint, but that the parts protected vary by message
      action.</span></p>
      <h2><a id="_Toc99269231" name="_Toc99269231"></a><a id="_Toc96244134"
      name="_Toc96244134"></a><a id="_Toc95877080" name="_Toc95877080"></a><a
      id="_Toc95113088" name="_Toc95113088"></a><a id="_Toc85362760"
      name="_Toc85362760"></a><a id="_Toc85350206" name="_Toc85350206"></a><a
      id="_Toc73939790" name="_Toc73939790"></a><a id="_Toc104103919"
      name="_Toc104103919"></a><a id="_12.2._License_Requirements"
      name="_12.2._License_Requirements"></a><a id="_12.3._Integrity_Requirements"
      name="_12.3._Integrity_Requirements"></a><a id="_2.3._Integrity_Requirements"
      name="_2.3._Integrity_Requirements"></a><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>2.2</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;<a id="_Toc161826505"
      name="_Toc161826505">Nested Policy Assertions</a></span></h2>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Assertions may be
      used to further qualify a specific aspect of another assertion. For example, an
      assertion describing the set of algorithms to use may qualify the specific behavior
      of a security binding. If the schema outline below for an assertion type requires a
      nested policy expression but the assertion does not further qualify one or more
      aspects of the behavior indicated by the assertion type (i.e., no assertions are
      needed in the nested policy expression), the assertion MUST include an empty
      &lt;wsp:Policy/&gt; element. For further information consult the section Policy
      Assertion Nesting of [WS-Policy].</span></p>
      <h2><a id="_Toc104103920" name="_Toc104103920"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>2.3</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></a><a id="_Toc161826506"
      name="_Toc161826506"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Security
      Binding Abstraction</span></a></h2>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>As previously
      indicated, individual assertions are designed to be used in multiple combinations.
      The binding represents common usage patterns for security mechanisms.&nbsp; These
      Security Binding assertions are used to determine how the security is performed and
      what to expect in the</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Courier New"'>wsse:Security</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>header.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Bindings are
      described textually and enforced programmatically.&nbsp; This specification defines
      several bindings but others can be defined and agreed to for interoperability if
      participating parties support it.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A binding defines
      the following security characteristics:</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in;text-indent:-.25in'><span
      style='font-family:Symbol'>&middot;<span
      style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</span></span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The minimum set of tokens that will
      be used and how they are bound to messages. Note that services might accept
      messages containing more tokens than those specified in policy</span><span
      xml:lang="EN-GB" style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>.</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in;text-indent:-.25in'><span
      style='font-family:Symbol'>&middot;<span
      style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</span></span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Any necessary key transport
      mechanisms</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in;text-indent:-.25in'><span
      style='font-family:Symbol'>&middot;<span
      style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</span></span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Any required message elements (e.g.
      timestamps) in the</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Courier New"'>wsse:Security</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>header.</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in;text-indent:-.25in'><span
      style='font-family:Symbol'>&middot;<span
      style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</span></span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The content and ordering of elements
      in the</span> <span style='font-family:"Courier New"'>wsse:Security</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>header. Elements not specified in the
      binding are not allowed.</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in;text-indent:-.25in'><span
      style='font-family:Symbol'>&middot;<span
      style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</span></span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Various parameters, including those
      describing the algorithms to be used for canonicalization, signing and
      encryption.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Together the above
      pieces of information, along with the assertions describing conditions and scope,
      provide enough information to secure messages between an initiator and a recipient.
      A policy consumer has enough information to construct messages that conform to the
      service's policy and to process messages returned by the service. Note that a
      service may choose to reject messages despite them conforming to its policy, for
      example because a client certificate has been revoked. Note also that a service may
      choose to accept messages that do not conform to its policy.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following list
      identifies the bindings defined in this specification.&nbsp; The bindings are
      identified primarily by the style of encryption used to protect the message
      exchange. A later section of this document provides details on the assertions for
      these bindings.</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:39.4pt;text-indent:-.25in'><span
      style='font-family:Symbol'>&middot;<span
      style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</span></span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>TransportBinding (Section
      7.3)</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:39.4pt;text-indent:-.25in'><span
      style='font-family:Symbol'>&middot;<span
      style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</span></span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>SymmetricBinding (Section
      7.4)</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:39.4pt;text-indent:-.25in'><span
      style='font-family:Symbol'>&middot;<span
      style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</span></span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>AsymmetricBinding (Section
      7.5)</span></p>
      <div style='border:none;border-top:solid gray 1.0pt;padding:6.0pt 0in 0in 0in'>
        <h1><a id="_Toc73939792" name="_Toc73939792"></a><a id="_Toc96244137"
        name="_Toc96244137"></a><a id="_Toc95877083" name="_Toc95877083"></a><a
        id="_Toc95113091" name="_Toc95113091"></a><a id="_Toc85362762"
        name="_Toc85362762"></a><a id="_Toc85350208" name="_Toc85350208"></a><a
        id="_Toc161826507" name="_Toc161826507"></a><a id="_Toc104103921"
        name="_Toc104103921"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>3<span
        style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</span></span>
        <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Policy
        Considerations</span></a></h1>
      </div>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
      sections discuss details of WS-Policy and WS-PolicyAttachment relevant to this
      specification.</span></p>
      <h2><a id="_Toc104103922" name="_Toc104103922"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>3.1</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></a><a id="_Toc161826508"
      name="_Toc161826508"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Nested
      Policy</span></a></h2>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This specification
      makes extensive use of nested policy assertions as described in the</span> <a
      href="http://www.w3.org/Submission/WS-Policy/#Policy_Assertion_Nesting"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Policy Assertion Nesting</span></a> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>section of WS-Policy.</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal"
      style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;line-height:12.0pt; text-autospace:none'>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <h2><a id="_Toc161826509" name="_Toc161826509"></a><a id="_Toc104103928"
      name="_Toc104103928"><span style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>3.2</span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Policy Subjects</span></a></h2>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>WS-PolicyAttachment
      defines various attachment points for policy. This section defines properties that
      are referenced later in this document describing the recommended or required
      attachment points for various assertions. In addition,</span> <a
      href="#AppendixA"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Appendix
      A</span></a> <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>groups the various
      assertions according to policy subject.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Note: This
      specification does not define any assertions that have a scope of [Service Policy
      Subject].</span></p>
      <p class="text0"><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>[Message Policy
      Subject]</span></b></p>
      <p class="text0"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This property
      identifies a Message Policy Subject [</span><a href="#wspolicyattachments"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>WS-PolicyAttachment</span></a><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>].</span> <span
      style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>WS-PolicyAttachment defines seven WSDL
      [WSDL 1.1] policy attachment points with Message Policy Subject:</span></p>
      <p class="text0"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:message</span></p>
      <p class="Definition"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A policy
      expression containing one or more assertions with Message Policy Subject MUST NOT
      be attached to a wsdl:message.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:portType/wsdl:operation/wsdl:input,
      ./wsdl:output, or ./wsdl:fault</span></p>
      <p class="Definition"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A policy
      expression containing one or more assertions with Message Policy Subject MUST NOT
      be attached to a descendant of wsdl:portType.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:binding/wsdl:operation/wsdl:input,
      ./wsdl:output, or ./wsdl:fault</span></p>
      <p class="Definition"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A policy
      expression containing one or more of the assertions with Message Policy Subject
      MUST be attached to a descendant of wsdl:binding.</span></p>
      <p class="text0"><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>[Operation
      Policy Subject]</span></b></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A token assertion
      with Operation Policy Subject indicates usage of the token on a per-operation
      basis:</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:portType/wsdl:operation</span></p>
      <p class="Definition"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A policy
      expression containing one or more token assertions MUST NOT be attached to a
      wsdl:portType/wsdl:operation.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:binding/wsdl:operation</span></p>
      <p class="Definition"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A policy
      expression containing one or more token assertions MUST be attached to a
      wsdl:binding/wsdl:operation.</span></p>
      <p class="Definition" style='margin-left:0in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Definitionterm"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="text0"><b><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>[Endpoint Policy
      Subject]</span></b></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A token assertion
      instance with Endpoint Policy Subject indicates usage of the token for the entire
      set of messages described for the endpoint:</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:portType</span></p>
      <p class="Definition"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A policy
      expression containing one or more assertions with Endpoint Policy Subject MUST NOT
      be attached to a wsdl:portType.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:binding</span></p>
      <p class="Definition"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A policy
      expression containing one or more of the assertions with Endpoint Policy Subject
      SHOULD be attached to a wsdl:binding.</span></p>
      <p class="DefinedTerm"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>wsdl:port</span></p>
      <p class="Definition"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A policy
      expression containing one or more of the assertions with Endpoint Policy Subject
      MAY be attached to a wsdl:port</span></p>
      <div style='border:none;border-top:solid gray 1.0pt;padding:6.0pt 0in 0in 0in'>
        <h1><a id="_Toc161826510" name="_Toc161826510"></a><a id="_Toc104103929"
        name="_Toc104103929"><span style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>4<span
        style='font:7.0pt "Times New Roman"'>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</span></span>
        <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Protection
        Assertions</span></a></h1>
      </div>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
      assertions are used to identify <i>what</i> is being protected and the level of
      protection provided. These assertions SHOULD apply to [Message Policy
      Subject].</span> <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>These assertions
      MAY apply to [Endpoint Policy Subject] or [Operation Policy Subject]. Where they
      apply to [Operation Policy Subject] they apply to all messages of that operation.
      Where they apply to [Endpoint Policy Subject] they apply to all operations of that
      endpoint.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Note that when
      assertions defined in this section are present in a policy, the order of those
      assertions in that policy has no effect on the order of signature and encryption
      operations (see Section 6.3).</span></p>
      <h2><a id="_Toc161826511" name="_Toc161826511"></a><a id="_Toc104103930"
      name="_Toc104103930"><span style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>4.1</span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Integrity Assertions</span></a></h2>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Two mechanisms are
      defined for specifying the set of message parts to integrity protect. One uses
      QNames to specify either message headers or the message body while the other uses
      XPath expressions to identify any part of the message.</span></p>
      <h3><a id="_Toc161826512" name="_Toc161826512"></a><a id="_Toc104103931"
      name="_Toc104103931"></a><a id="_Toc96244138" name="_Toc96244138"></a><a
      id="_Toc95877084" name="_Toc95877084"></a><a id="_Toc95113092"
      name="_Toc95113092"></a><a id="_Toc85362763" name="_Toc85362763"></a><a
      id="_Toc85350209" name="_Toc85350209"></a><a id="_SignedParts_Assertion"
      name="_SignedParts_Assertion"></a><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>4.1.1</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>SignedParts Assertion</span></h3>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The SignedParts
      assertion is used to specify the parts of the message outside of security headers
      that require integrity protection. This assertion can be satisfied using WSS: SOAP
      Message Security mechanisms or by mechanisms out of scope of SOAP message security,
      for example by sending the message over a secure transport protocol like
      HTTPS.&nbsp; The binding specific token properties detail the exact mechanism by
      which the protection is provided.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>There MAY be
      multiple SignedParts assertions present. Multiple SignedParts assertions present
      within a policy alternative are equivalent to a single SignedParts assertion
      containing the union of all specified message parts. Note that this assertion does
      not require that a given part appear in a message, just that if such a part
      appears, it requires integrity protection.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><b><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Syntax</span></b></p>
      <div
      style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom: solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in; background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>
        <p class="Code" style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>
        &lt;sp:SignedParts xmlns:sp="..." ... &gt;<br />
        &nbsp; &lt;sp:Body /&gt;?<br />
        &nbsp; &lt;sp:Header Name="<i>xs:NCName</i>"? Namespace="<i>xs:anyURI</i>" ...
        /&gt;*</p>
        <p class="Code" style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>
        &nbsp; &lt;sp:Attachments /&gt;?<br />
        &nbsp; ...<br />
        &lt;/sp:SignedParts&gt;</p>
      </div>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
      describes the attributes and elements listed in the schema outlined
      above:</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedParts</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This assertion specifies the parts of the
      message that need integrity protection. If no child elements are specified, all
      message headers targeted at the UltimateReceiver role [SOAP12] or actor [SOAP11]
      and the body of the message MUST be integrity protected.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedParts/sp:Body</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Presence of this optional empty element
      indicates that the entire body, that is the soap:Body element, it's attributes and
      content, of the message needs to be integrity protected.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedParts/sp:Header</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Presence of this optional element
      indicates a specific SOAP header, its attributes and content (or set of such
      headers) needs to be protected. There may be multiple sp:Header elements within a
      single sp:SignedParts element. If multiple SOAP headers with the same local name
      but different namespace names are to be integrity protected multiple sp:Header
      elements are needed, either as part of a single sp:SignedParts assertion or as part
      of separate sp:SignedParts assertions.<br />
      This element only applies to SOAP header elements targeted to the same actor/role
      as the Security header impacted by the policy. If it is necessary to specify a
      requirement to sign specific SOAP Header elements targeted to a different
      actor/role, that may be accomplished using the sp:SignedElements
      assertion.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedParts/sp:Header/@Name</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This optional attribute indicates the
      local name of the SOAP header to be integrity protected. If this attribute is not
      specified, all SOAP headers whose namespace matches the Namespace attribute are to
      be protected.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedParts/sp:Header/@Namespace</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This required attribute indicates the
      namespace of the SOAP header(s) to be integrity protected.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedParts/sp:Attachments</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Presence of this optional empty element
      indicates that all SwA (SOAP Messages with Attachments) attachments [SwA] are to be
      integrity protected. When SOAP Message Security is used to accomplish this, all
      message parts other than the part containing the primary SOAP envelope are to be
      integrity protected as outlined in WSS: SOAP Message Security
      [WSS:SwAProfile1.1].</span></p>
      <h3><a id="_Toc96244139" name="_Toc96244139"></a><a id="_Toc95877085"
      name="_Toc95877085"></a><a id="_Toc95113093" name="_Toc95113093"></a><a
      id="_Toc85362764" name="_Toc85362764"></a><a id="_Toc85350210"
      name="_Toc85350210"></a><a id="_Toc161826513" name="_Toc161826513"></a><a
      id="_Toc104103932" name="_Toc104103932"></a><a id="_SignedElements_Assertion"
      name="_SignedElements_Assertion"></a><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>4.1.2</span> <span
      style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>SignedElements Assertion</span></h3>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The SignedElements
      assertion is used to specify arbitrary elements in the message that require
      integrity protection. This assertion can be satisfied using WSS: SOAP Message
      Security mechanisms or by mechanisms out of scope of SOAP message security, for
      example by sending the message over a secure transport protocol like HTTPS.&nbsp;
      The binding specific token properties detail the exact mechanism by which the
      protection is provided.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>There MAY be
      multiple SignedElements assertions present. Multiple SignedElements assertions
      present within a policy alternative are equivalent to a single SignedElements
      assertion containing the union of all specified XPath expressions.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><b><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Syntax</span></b></p>
      <div
      style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom: solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in; background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>
        <p class="Code" style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>
        &lt;sp:SignedElements XPathVersion="<i>xs:anyURI</i>"? xmlns:sp="..." ...
        &gt;<br />
        &nbsp; &lt;sp:XPath&gt;<i>xs:string</i>&lt;/sp:XPath&gt;+<br />
        &nbsp; ...<br />
        &lt;/sp:SignedElements&gt;</p>
      </div>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
      describes the attributes and elements listed in the schema outlined
      above:</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedElements</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This assertion specifies the parts of the
      message that need integrity protection.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedElements/@XPathVersion</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This optional attribute contains a URI
      which indicates the version of XPath to use. If no attribute is provided, then
      XPath 1.0 is assumed.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:SignedElements/sp:XPath</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This element contains a string specifying
      an XPath expression that identifies the nodes to be integrity protected. The XPath
      expression is evaluated against the S:Envelope element node of the message.
      Multiple instances of this element may appear within this assertion and should be
      treated as separate references in a signature when message security is
      used.</span></p>
      <h2><a id="_Toc161826514" name="_Toc161826514"></a><a id="_Toc104103933"
      name="_Toc104103933"><span style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>4.2</span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Confidentiality
      Assertions</span></a></h2>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Two mechanisms are
      defined for specifying the set of message parts to confidentiality protect. One
      uses QNames to specify either message headers or the message body while the other
      uses XPath expressions to identify any part of the message.</span></p>
      <h3><a id="_Toc161826515" name="_Toc161826515"></a><a id="_Toc104103934"
      name="_Toc104103934"></a><a id="_EncryptedParts_Assertion"
      name="_EncryptedParts_Assertion"></a><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>4.2.1</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>EncryptedParts Assertion</span></h3>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The EncryptedParts
      assertion is used to specify the parts of the message that require confidentiality.
      This assertion can be satisfied with WSS: SOAP Message Security mechanisms or by
      mechanisms out of scope of SOAP message security, for example by sending the
      message over a secure transport protocol like HTTPS.&nbsp; The binding specific
      token properties detail the exact mechanism by which the protection is
      provided.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>There MAY be
      multiple EncryptedParts assertions present. Multiple EncryptedParts assertions
      present within a policy alternative are equivalent to a single EncryptedParts
      assertion containing the union of all specified message parts. Note that this
      assertion does not require that a given part appear in a message, just that if such
      a part appears, it requires confidentiality protection.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><b><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Syntax</span></b></p>
      <div
      style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom: solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in; background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>
        <p class="Code" style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>
        &lt;sp:EncryptedParts xmlns:sp="..." ... &gt;<br />
        &nbsp; &lt;sp:Body/&gt;?<br />
        &nbsp; &lt;sp:Header Name="<i>xs:NCName</i>"? Namespace="<i>xs:anyURI</i>" ...
        /&gt;*</p>
        <p class="Code" style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>
        &nbsp; &lt;sp:Attachments /&gt;?<br />
        &nbsp; ...<br />
        &lt;/sp:EncryptedParts&gt;</p>
      </div>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
      describes the attributes and elements listed in the schema outlined
      above:</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedParts</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This assertion specifies the parts of the
      message that need confidentiality protection. The single child element of this
      assertion specifies the set of message parts using an extensible
      dialect.</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>If no child elements are specified, the
      body of the message MUST be confidentiality protected.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedParts/sp:Body</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Presence of this optional empty element
      indicates that the entire body of the message needs to be confidentiality
      protected. In the case where mechanisms from WSS: SOAP Message Security are used to
      satisfy this assertion, then the soap:Body element is encrypted using the #Content
      encryption type.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedParts/sp:Header</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Presence of this optional element
      indicates that a specific SOAP header (or set of such headers) needs to be
      protected. There may be multiple sp:Header elements within a single Parts element.
      Each header or set of headers MUST be encrypted. Such encryption will encrypt such
      elements using WSS 1.1 Encrypted Headers. As such, if WSS 1.1 Encrypted Headers are
      not supported by a service, then this element cannot be used to specify headers
      that require encryption using message level security. If multiple SOAP headers with
      the same local name but different namespace names are to be encrypted then multiple
      sp:Header elements are needed, either as part of a single sp:EncryptedParts
      assertion or as part of separate sp:EncryptedParts assertions.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedParts/sp:Header/@Name</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This optional attribute indicates the
      local name of the SOAP header to be confidentiality protected. If this attribute is
      not specified, all SOAP headers whose namespace matches the Namespace attribute are
      to be protected.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedParts/sp:Header/@Namespace</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This required attribute indicates the
      namespace of the SOAP header(s) to be confidentiality protected.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedParts/sp:Attachments</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Presence of this optional empty element
      indicates that all SwA (SOAP Messages with Attachments) attachments [SwA] are to be
      confidentiality protected. When SOAP Message Security is used to accomplish this,
      all message parts other than the part containing the primary SOAP envelope are to
      be confidentiality protected as outlined in WSS: SOAP Message Security
      [WSS:SwAProfile1.1].</span></p>
      <h3><a id="_Toc96244140" name="_Toc96244140"></a><a id="_Toc95877086"
      name="_Toc95877086"></a><a id="_Toc95113100" name="_Toc95113100"></a><a
      id="_Toc92518192" name="_Toc92518192"></a><a id="_Toc161826516"
      name="_Toc161826516"></a><a id="_Toc104103935" name="_Toc104103935"></a><a
      id="_EncryptedElements_Assertion" name="_EncryptedElements_Assertion"></a><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>4.2.2</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>EncryptedElements Assertion</span></h3>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The
      EncryptedElements assertion is used to specify arbitrary elements in the message
      that require confidentiality protection. This assertion can be satisfied using WSS:
      SOAP Message Security mechanisms or by mechanisms out of scope of SOAP message
      security, for example by sending the message over a secure transport protocol like
      HTTPS.&nbsp; The binding specific token properties detail the exact mechanism by
      which the protection is provided.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>There MAY be
      multiple EncryptedElements assertions present. Multiple EncryptedElements
      assertions present within a policy alternative are equivalent to a single
      EncryptedElements assertion containing the union of all specified XPath
      expressions.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><b><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Syntax</span></b></p>
      <div
      style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom: solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in; background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>
        <p class="Code" style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>
        &lt;sp:EncryptedElements XPathVersion="<i>xs:anyURI</i>"? xmlns:sp="..." ...
        &gt;<br />
        &nbsp; &lt;sp:XPath&gt;<i>xs:string</i>&lt;/sp:XPath&gt;+<br />
        &nbsp; ...<br />
        &lt;/sp:EncryptedElements&gt;</p>
      </div>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
      describes the attributes and elements listed in the schema outlined
      above:</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedElements</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This assertion specifies the parts of the
      message that need confidentiality protection. Any such elements are subject to
      #Element encryption.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedElements/@XPathVersion</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This optional attribute contains a URI
      which indicates the version of XPath to use. If no attribute is provided, then
      XPath 1.0 is assumed.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:EncryptedElements/sp:XPath</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This element contains a string specifying
      an XPath expression that identifies the nodes to be confidentiality protected. The
      XPath expression is evaluated against the S:Envelope element node of the message.
      Multiple instances of this element may appear within this assertion and should be
      treated as separate references.</span></p>
      <h3><a id="_Toc161826517" name="_Toc161826517"></a><a
      id="_ContentEncryptedElements_Assertion"
      name="_ContentEncryptedElements_Assertion"></a><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>4.2.3</span> <span
      style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>ContentEncryptedElements
      Assertion</span></h3>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The
      ContentEncryptedElements assertion is used to specify arbitrary elements in the
      message that require confidentiality protection of their content. This assertion
      can be satisfied using WSS: SOAP Message Security mechanisms or by mechanisms out
      of scope of SOAP message security, for example by sending the message over a secure
      transport protocol like HTTPS.&nbsp; The binding specific token properties detail
      the exact mechanism by which the protection is provided.</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>There MAY be
      multiple ContentEncryptedElements assertions present. Multiple
      ContentEncryptedElements assertions present within a policy alternative are
      equivalent to a single ContentEncryptedElements assertion containing the union of
      all specified XPath expressions.</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><b><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Syntax</span></b></p>
      <div
      style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom: solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in; background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>
        <p class="Code" style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>
        &lt;sp:ContentEncryptedElements XPathVersion="<i>xs:anyURI</i>"? ...&gt;<br />
        &nbsp; &lt;sp:XPath&gt;<i>xs:string</i>&lt;/sp:XPath&gt;+<br />
        &nbsp; ...<br />
        &lt;/sp:ContentEncryptedElements&gt;</p>
      </div>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
      describes the attributes and elements listed in the schema outlined
      above:</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:ContentEncryptedElements</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This assertion specifies the parts of the
      message that need confidentiality protection. Any such elements are subject to
      #Content encryption.</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:ContentEncryptedElements/@XPathVersion</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This optional attribute contains a URI
      which indicates the version of XPath to use.</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:ContentEncryptedElements/sp:XPath</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This element contains a string specifying
      an XPath expression that identifies the nodes to be confidentiality protected. The
      XPath expression is evaluated against the S:Envelope element node of the message.
      Multiple instances of this element MAY appear within this assertion and should be
      treated as separate references.</span></p>
      <h2><a id="_Toc161826518" name="_Toc161826518"></a><a id="_Toc104103936"
      name="_Toc104103936"><span style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>4.3</span>
      <span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Required Elements
      Assertion</span></a></h2>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>A mechanism is
      defined for specifying, using XPath expressions, the set of header elements that a
      message MUST contain.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Note: Specifications
      are expected to provide domain specific assertions that specify which headers are
      expected in a message. This assertion is provided for cases where such domain
      specific assertions have not been defined.</span></p>
      <h3><a id="_Toc161826519" name="_Toc161826519"></a><a id="_Toc104103937"
      name="_Toc104103937"></a><a id="_RequiredElements_Assertion"
      name="_RequiredElements_Assertion"></a><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>4.3.1</span> <span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>RequiredElements Assertion</span></h3>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The RequiredElements
      assertion is used to specify header elements that the message MUST contain. This
      assertion specifies no security requirements.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>There MAY be
      multiple RequiredElements assertions present. Multiple RequiredElements assertions
      present within a policy alternative are equivalent to a single RequiredElements
      assertion containing the union of all specified XPath expressions.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><b><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Syntax</span></b></p>
      <div
      style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom: solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in; background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>
        <p class="Code" style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>
        &lt;sp:RequiredElements XPathVersion="<i>xs:anyURI</i>"? xmlns:sp="..." ...
        &gt;<br />
        &nbsp; &lt;sp:XPath&gt;<i>xs:string</i>&lt;/sp:XPath&gt; +<br />
        &nbsp; ...<br />
        &lt;/sp:RequiredElements&gt;</p>
      </div>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
      describes the attributes and elements listed in the schema outlined
      above:</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:RequiredElements</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This assertion specifies the headers
      elements that MUST appear in a message.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:RequiredElements/@XPathVersion</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This optional attribute contains a URI
      which indicates the version of XPath to use. If no attribute is provided, then
      XPath 1.0 is assumed.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:RequiredElements/sp:XPath</span></p>
      <p class="Text" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This element contains a string specifying
      an XPath expression that identifies the header elements that a message MUST
      contain. The XPath expression is evaluated against the S:Envelope/S:Header element
      node of the message. Multiple instances of this element may appear within this
      assertion and should be treated as a combined XPath expression.</span></p>
      <h3><a id="_Toc161826520" name="_Toc161826520"></a><a id="_RequiredParts_Assertion"
      name="_RequiredParts_Assertion"></a><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>4.3.2</span> <span
      style='font-family: "Arial","sans-serif"'>RequiredParts Assertion</span></h3>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>RequiredParts
      is a QName based alternative to the RequiredElements assertion (which is based on
      XPATH) for specifying header elements that MUST be present in the message. This
      assertion specifies no security requirements.</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>There MAY be
      multiple RequiredParts assertions present. Multiple RequiredParts assertions
      present within a policy alternative are equivalent to a single RequiredParts
      assertion containing the union of all specified Header elements.</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><b><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>Syntax</span></b></p>
      <div
      style='border-top:solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-left:none;border-bottom: solid windowtext 1.0pt;border-right:none;padding:3.0pt 0in 3.0pt 0in; background:#D9D9D9;margin-left:.3in;margin-right:.3in'>
        <p class="Code" style='margin:0in;margin-bottom:.0001pt;background:#D9D9D9'>
        &lt;sp:RequiredParts XPathVersion="<i>xs:anyURI</i>"? xmlns:sp="..." ...
        &gt;<br />
        &nbsp; &lt;sp:Header&nbsp; Name ="..."&nbsp; Namespace= "..." /&gt; +<br />
        &lt;/sp:RequiredParts&gt;</p>
      </div>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>&nbsp;</span></p>
      <p class="Text"><span style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>The following
      describes the attributes and elements listed in the schema outlined
      above:</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:RequiredParts/sp:Header</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This assertion specifies the headers
      elements that MUST be present in the message.</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal"><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>/sp:RequiredParts/sp:Header/@Name</span></p>
      <p class="MsoNormal" style='margin-left:.5in'><span
      style='font-family:"Arial","sans-serif"'>This required attribute indicates the
